Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. Hi, Doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. I know you're very busy. I'm... well, I'm probably just overreacting, honestly. I've just been a little stressed lately, I think. But my husband thought it would be a good idea for me to come in, so here I am. 

I guess... well, what actually scared me enough to make the appointment is... it's a little silly, but I've been hearing my name called. Like, from the hallway at home. Oh, it's very fast. Just a split second, honestly. Like I was saying before about when it happened in the hallway or that one time at the warehouse... it's literally just my name. So it only lasts however long it takes to say that.

Well, um... I guess maybe a little bit? But it's really nothing to worry about, I promise. It's just... well, sometimes my mind feels like it's going a little fast. 

Like, I'll be at work thinking about the warehouse orders, and then suddenly I'm thinking about the colors of yarn I need to buy for a blanket, and then I'm worrying about the electric bill, and then I'm trying to remember the names of my great-aunts... and it just jumps from one thing to the next so quickly. Sometimes I even forget what I was actually trying to do in the first place because my brain is just zooming around so much. 

But honestly, I think it's just because I have a lot on my plate right now. Everyone gets a little scattered when they're busy, right? I'm sure it's completely normal to have your thoughts jump around a bit when you're trying to keep track of everything. I'm doing okay, aren't I?

Well, um... I guess maybe a little bit? But I think it's just from being so worn out, you know? After all that running around and... and arguing with people, I just sort of... crashed.

But I'm sure it's just exhaustion, right? Anyone would feel a little down after going a million miles an hour. It doesn't mean anything bad about me, does it? I'm trying really hard to just push through it.

It just means my brain is a little tired for a second?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your thoughts or the way you think about things lately?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess maybe a little bit? But it's really nothing to worry about, I promise. It's just... well, sometimes my mind feels like it's going a little fast. \n\nLike, I'll be at work thinking about the warehouse orders, and then suddenly I'm thinking about the colors of yarn I need to buy for a blanket, and then I'm worrying about the electric bill, and then I'm trying to remember the names of my great-aunts... and it just jumps from one thing to the next so quickly. Sometimes I even forget what I was actually trying to do in the first place because my brain is just zooming around so much. \n\nBut honestly, I think it's just because I have a lot on my plate right now. Well, um... I guess I've been a little less active with my hobbies lately. But I think that's normal when you're tired, right? 

I usually love my knitting basket and tending to my garden beds, but... I just haven't touched them in a while. And I haven't been to my book club or signed up for the church food pantry, either. It's just... even the things I usually care so much about, they just feel like too much trouble right now. Like they aren't really worth doing. 

But I'm sure that's just a phase, right? I mean, everyone needs a break from their routine sometimes. I don't want you to think I'm lazy or anything—I'm usually very involved! I just need to catch my breath, don't you think?

Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.     "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I'm sorry, I feel like I'm repeating myself, and I really don't want to waste your time... but, yes, I suppose I have. 

Like I was saying, my knitting basket is just sitting there gathering dust, and I haven't been out to the garden beds at all. And the book club... well, I haven't gone to that, or the church food pantry either. I know it sounds terrible, and I really am usually such a dedicated person! It's just... lately, even the things I care about the most, they just feel like too much trouble. It's like my brain is telling me it's not worth doing, even though my heart knows it is. 

But I'm sure you see this all the time, right? It's just a little slump. I'm not... I'm not a lost cause or anything, am I? I really am trying my best to snap out of it.

I'm not a lost cause or anything, am I? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh dear, I must be doing a terrible job of explaining things, I am so sorry. I don't mean to make you ask the same thing over and over... I must be frustrating you. 

But, um... yes. I guess if I'm being completely honest, it really is almost everything right now. Like I was saying about my knitting basket and the garden beds, and the book club and the church food pantry... it's just that all of it feels like way too much trouble. Even the things I usually care about the most, they just don't feel worth doing at the moment. 

I know that sounds so awful and lazy, and I'm really ashamed to admit it out loud. I promise I'm usually a very hard worker at my job and at home! You don't think terribly of me for letting things go like this, do you? I really am trying to give you the right answers.

Like I was saying, it really was just that one single time at the warehouse when the aisle was completely empty, and then from the hallway at my house. \n\nI haven't heard it out at the grocery store or running errands or anything like that. It just... it really spooked me because of how incredibly clear it was. I swear it sounded exactly like my husband was standing right next to me, even when no one was there. But since it's really just been at home and work, it's definitely just fatigue, don't you think? I hope I'm not wasting your time with all this.\n\nDoctor: Have you been seeing, smelling, or feeling anything that isn't actually there, besides hearing your name called?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. No, nothing like that at all. I haven't seen anything strange or... or smelled anything that wasn't there. And I definitely haven't felt anything touching me. \n\nI know hearing my name was weird enough, but I promise it's just the hearing part, and even that is so rare. I'm completely fine otherwise. I would tell you if there was anything else, I really would. I just want to make sure I'm giving you all the right information... I'm doing okay, right? It's normal to just have that one little thing happen when you're stressed?\n\nDoctor: How long does the hearing of your name last when it happens?\n\nPatient: Oh, it's very fast. Just a split second, honestly. Like I was saying before about when it happened in the hallway or that one time at the warehouse... it's literally just my name. So it only lasts however long it takes to say that. \n\nIt's just a quick call, exactly like if my husband was standing right next to me and just said my name to get my attention. Since it's over so quickly, that's a good sign, right? It just means my brain is a little tired for a second?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your thoughts or the way you think about things lately?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess maybe a little bit? But it's really nothing to worry about, I promise. It's just... well, sometimes my mind feels like it's going a little fast. \n\nLike, I'll be at work thinking about the warehouse orders, and then suddenly I'm thinking about the colors of yarn I need to buy for a blanket, and then I'm worrying about the electric bill, and then I'm trying to remember the names of my great-aunts... and it just jumps from one thing to the next so quickly. Sometimes I even forget what I was actually trying to do in the first place because my brain is just zooming around so much. \n\nBut honestly, I think it's just because I have a lot on my plate right now. Everyone gets a little scattered when they're busy, right? I'm sure it's completely normal to have your thoughts jump around a bit when you're trying to keep track of everything. I'm doing okay, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself having trouble expressing your thoughts or finding the right words when talking to someone?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, not really. I mean, I think I talk just fine. Sometimes I might talk a little fast when my thoughts are going quickly, like I mentioned, but I don't get stuck on words or anything like that. \n\nI can always get my point across, even if I have a lot to say all at once. My husband always says I can talk the ear off a brass monkey, so finding words definitely isn't a problem for me. I'm communicating clearly with you, right? I want to make sure I'm making sense and not rambling too much.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are not making sense or are disconnected from each other?\n\nPatient: No, no, I wouldn't say that. My thoughts make sense to me, at least. Like I said, they might jump from the warehouse to the yarn to the bills, but there's always a reason for it, you know? It's just my brain trying to handle all my responsibilities at once. \n\nIt's not like they're just... random nonsense or anything. I'm just a busy person trying to keep all the plates spinning. I mean, I'm sure you hear people say things that are completely disconnected all the time, but I'm not like that, right? I'm keeping my thoughts pretty organized for you today, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your mood lately?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess maybe a little bit? But I think it's just from being so worn out, you know? After all that running around and... and arguing with people, I just sort of... crashed. \n\nIt's silly, really, but lately I just drop into this heavy, empty feeling. I'll just sit at the kitchen table and cry over absolutely nothing. It's like I just can't get myself back to normal. But I'm sure it's just exhaustion, right? Anyone would feel a little down after going a million miles an hour. It doesn't mean anything bad about me, does it? I'm trying really hard to just push through it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these heavy, empty feelings?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I suppose it's been a little while now. Maybe... about three months? But it wasn't always this bad, I promise. It's just sort of been hanging over me, this quiet, withdrawn feeling. \n\nIt really only got harder to manage these past few weeks, after everything got so intense. I usually do such a good job of keeping it together, you know? I don't want you to think I'm just giving up or anything. I've been trying so hard to shake it off. Three months isn't too terribly long to feel a bit down, is it? I'm sure I'll bounce back soon.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying the things you usually like to do?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I've been a little less active with my hobbies lately. But I think that's normal when you're tired, right? \n\nI usually love my knitting basket and tending to my garden beds, but... I just haven't touched them in a while. And I haven't been to my book club or signed up for the church food pantry, either. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, well, um... my weight? I haven't been on a scale lately, to be perfectly honest. I know I should keep better track of these things, I'm sorry. 

But my appetite has just been... well, it's been a little all over the place. When I had all that energy before, those times I was running around planning things, I would just completely forget to eat. I was just so busy, you know? And then lately, with this heavy feeling... I just sort of pick at a piece of toast here and there. Nothing really sounds good. 

I know I need to eat properly to keep my strength up for work, and I really am trying to do better with it. I'm not doing it on purpose to be difficult or anything. Do you think a little change in appetite is normal when you're feeling this down? I hope I'm not causing any permanent damage by not eating right...

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh... um. I really hate to even say it out loud, because I know how awful it sounds. I don't want you to think badly of me, or think I'm... well, ungrateful for my life. 

But... sometimes, with how heavy everything feels lately, I just keep having these thoughts that maybe... maybe it might just be easier if I didn't wake up. 

It scares me so much when my mind goes there. I really don't want to be that kind of person, I promise you. And I want to be completely clear, I haven't made any sort of plan to actually hurt myself! I would never, ever want to do something like that. I'm just so tired of feeling this way. It's somewhat normal to have those passing thoughts when you're this exhausted, right? You don't think I'm a terrible person for thinking that way, do you? I really am trying my best to stay positive.

Three months isn't too terribly long to feel a bit down, is it? I'm sure I'll bounce back soon.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying the things you usually like to do?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I've been a little less active with my hobbies lately. But I think that's normal when you're tired, right? \n\nI usually love my knitting basket and tending to my garden beds, but... I just haven't touched them in a while. And I haven't been to my book club or signed up for the church food pantry, either. It's just... even the things I usually care so much about, they just feel like too much trouble right now. Like they aren't really worth doing. \n\nBut I'm sure that's just a phase, right? I mean, everyone needs a break from their routine sometimes. I don't want you to think I'm lazy or anything—I'm usually very involved! I just need to catch my breath, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to enjoy doing, but now you don't find pleasure in them anymore?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I know I just mentioned this, but yes, I guess that's true. I just... I haven't even looked at my knitting basket in weeks, and my garden beds are probably a mess by now. \n\nAnd, um, I haven't been to my book club or even looked at the sign-up sheet for the church food pantry. It's awful of me, I know. I usually love all of that, I really do! But lately... it all just feels like so much trouble. Like it's just not worth the effort, even though I know it should be. \n\nThat's just the tiredness talking, though, isn't it? I'm not a bad person for needing a little break from everything, am I? I promise I'll get back to it all as soon as I have my energy back.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I'm sorry, I feel like I'm repeating myself, and I really don't want to waste your time... but, yes, I suppose I have. \n\nLike I was saying, my knitting basket is just sitting there gathering dust, and I haven't been out to the garden beds at all. And the book club... well, I haven't gone to that, or the church food pantry either. I know it sounds terrible, and I really am usually such a dedicated person! It's just... lately, even the things I care about the most, they just feel like too much trouble. It's like my brain is telling me it's not worth doing, even though my heart knows it is. \n\nBut I'm sure you see this all the time, right? It's just a little slump. I'm not... I'm not a lost cause or anything, am I? I really am trying my best to snap out of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost pleasure in almost all activities most of the time?\n\nPatient: Oh dear, I must be doing a terrible job of explaining things, I am so sorry. I don't mean to make you ask the same thing over and over... I must be frustrating you. \n\nBut, um... yes. I guess if I'm being completely honest, it really is almost everything right now. Like I was saying about my knitting basket and the garden beds, and the book club and the church food pantry... it's just that all of it feels like way too much trouble. Even the things I usually care about the most, they just don't feel worth doing at the moment. \n\nI know that sounds so awful and lazy, and I'm really ashamed to admit it out loud. I promise I'm usually a very hard worker at my job and at home! You don't think terribly of me for letting things go like this, do you? I want to make sure I give you the right timeline.
